It's mostly just an issue at the lab.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself getting distracted or having trouble focusing during everyday activities at home, like watching TV, talking with friends, or doing something you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... like I just said, I haven't really noticed it being a problem at home. I don't watch much TV anyway. And I... I don't really have people over. \n\nWhen I'm home, I just... I'm just there. I'm not really doing anything that requires focus, so... I wouldn't really know if I was distracted.\n\nDoctor: When you get interested in something, like an experiment or a specific idea, do you find yourself focusing on it so intensely that you lose track of time or don't notice what's going on around you?\n\nPatient: I guess... maybe? Sometimes I'll get caught up in looking at data or... trying to figure out why a piece of equipment is acting up. \n\nBut I don't know if it's... I mean, I'm just working. It's my job to figure those things out. I don't really know if I lose track of time more than anyone else would.\n\nDoctor: When you're deeply engaged in something that interests you, like an experiment or a technical problem, do you find it extremely hard to shift your attention away from it, even if someone tries to interrupt or if it's time to move on to something else?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really think so. I mean, if someone talks to me, I hear them. If I need to go do something else, I just go do it. It's just work. I don't think it's that hard to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel restless, down, or unmotivated when you're not focused on something that really interests you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean... I'm just normal, I guess. I don't really pay attention to how I feel when I'm not doing anything. I just exist. I don't think I feel any particular way about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling unusually sad, hopeless, or losing interest in things you used to enjoy, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't... I wouldn't say I feel hopeless. I just go home and do what needs to be done. I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like you're not enjoying things that used to make you happy, even if just for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: I... I think I just answered that. No. Like I said, I just do my normal routine. I don't really sit around analyzing if I'm happy or not.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you find it difficult to engage in social interactions, pick up on social cues, or feel interested in connecting with others?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean... I talk to people when I need to. I don't have trouble understanding them or anything. I just prefer to keep to myself mostly, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you have any hobbies or activities that you regularly engage in for enjoyment, and do you find yourself avoiding them or not getting as much out of them lately?\n\nPatient: I don't really have hobbies. Um... not in the way you mean, I guess. I just read sometimes, or watch the news. It hasn't changed. I'm not avoiding anything, I just do what I always do.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking with someone, do you find it hard to share your feelings or respond to theirs in the way they expect?\n\nPatient: I don't really... I mean, I'm not the type to talk about my feelings anyway. People don't really expect me to. If someone says something to me, I just respond how anyone would, I guess. I haven't noticed it being hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself having thoughts that keep coming into your mind, even when you don't want them there, or feel like you have to do certain things over and over again to feel okay?\n\nPatient: No. I don't really have anything like that. My thoughts are just... my thoughts. I don't have to do things over and over.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things in your life, like work responsibilities, relationships, or other everyday issues—even when you're not directly dealing with them?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I think about work sometimes, but just normal stuff. I don't sit around worrying about it. It's just work.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel down, even for a little while, or find that you don't take pleasure in things you used to?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... sometimes I'm tired, but that's normal. I don't think I feel down, exactly. Just... doing what I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like you don't have the motivation to do even simple things, like getting up or going out for a bit?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just do what needs to be done.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like you don't care about what's going on around you, or that you don't feel much pleasure or interest in anything at all?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I mean, I still care about things. I just... I go to work, I come home. It's just a normal routine. I wouldn't say I don't care.\n\nDoctor: Outside of work, do you ever feel like things that used to be enjoyable don’t seem to bring you any real pleasure anymore, even small things like a good meal or some time outside?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I don't really think about whether it brings me pleasure or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
